./docs/06-development-plan.md:3:*Status: v1 plan, 2026-09-08 — **Q-0110 shipped, and two of its three alleged defects were ruled correct**: an exit code reports a run's disposition rather than its verdict, so `regressed` keeps its zero, and bare `quorum adapters` is a report where `--probe` is the check. Only the unknown-command zero changed. **Q-0111 shipped**, implemented by hand the day after the entry that is its requirement: `core` states the condition, `packages/cli` composes the remedy once, and the sentence a stranger sees is byte-identical. Two of its three moved guards went red on themselves before they shipped, one of them matching nothing at all including its own subject. 2026-09-07 — **two decision entries landed and Q-0082 closed on one of them**. *"A deferral is not a skip"* answers neither of the two readings that ticket offered: the skipped-subject rule is not amended because it does not reach, and no code changes — decided on a measurement neither the ticket nor its two predecessor accounts had, that **every chore run this repository has ever performed deferred a range silently**, some sixty of them, because `chore.yaml` diffs against a branch its own earlier step creates. *"A `core` error names the condition; the remedy belongs to the surface"* rules Q-0111 and disproves that ticket's own cost argument: all six catch sites are byte-identical `die(error.message)`, so the remedy lands at one site rather than six. **Q-0108 shipped by hand**, the first ticket closed here for the reason that the gate costs more than the work: one clause plus two registrations. Its ruling is that a suite may **read** `CLAUDE.md`, Q-0103 E-2 having made it the human's to **write** and the two being different acts — recorded in the check's own authority comment, with no decision entry owed. **It refuted its own prediction**: the new read did not earn a `turbo-inputs.test.ts` registration, because `pathLiterals` skips any literal with no `/` and so never considers a root-level file at all — twelve of them, invisible to the scanner, covered only by a hand-audited register that fails open for a thirteenth. Reported, not fixed. **Three obligations recorded in prose are now tickets: Q-0110, Q-0111 and Q-0112.** The third is the `owner` default, which `ticket.ts:20` had routed to an unnamed successor since Q-0093 and which **fired twice while the first two were being opened**. Writing it up measured something none of its nine earlier sightings had: `--owner` with no value writes the boolean `true`, and `ticketSchema` declares `owner: z.string()` and refuses it — so `quorum ticket new` can write a ticket the product's own schema rejects, and `quorum board` renders it without noticing. **The two orphaned obligations are tickets: Q-0110 and Q-0111.** Q-0090's GA-4 said *"open the successor"* and nobody did; Q-0100 wrote its question into a `project.ts` JSDoc and into no ticket. They are **two** rather than one, measured rather than merged on the pattern the two same-day merges set: different packages, different decisions, and different forcing functions — a shell script that cannot tell *did the thing* from *did not understand you*, against M3's server surfacing a shell imperative to a browser. **Q-0110 is one wider than GA-4 named**, because a third zero was routed to it later by a source comment, so the obligation grew in the code after the instruction that created it. Creating them reproduced a live preserved defect on the spot: `quorum ticket new` stamped `owner: ruudvanengelenhoven` on both from `process.env.USER` (`backlog.ts:190`), **instances ten and eleven**, corrected by hand as the fourth such correction and still not reaching the code. **Q-0066 was absorbed into Q-0068**, which is retitled and raised to `p2`, and **Q-0109 into Q-0074**. The two merges rest on different evidence and the difference is the point: Q-0074/Q-0109 were literally one function declared twice, while Q-0066/Q-0068 are two unlike defects on **one surface** — `packages/cli/src/adapters.ts`'s own module header names both tickets in one docblock, the combined file set is eleven files that are the same eleven, one gate rules both questions and neither owes a decision entry. **A third defect on that same command has no ticket**: `quorum adapters` exits 0 with both CLIs absent, whose successor is named as *"Q-0090's GA-4"* — an instruction to open a ticket that lives only inside Q-0090's own merged requirement, never acted on, with Q-0090 closed since 2026-09-02. **That is the second orphaned obligation found this week**, after Q-0100's imperative living only in a `project.ts` JSDoc, and together they are a seventh direction of this page's drift: *an obligation recorded inside a closed ticket rather than as a ticket*. Q-0105 is the counter-example that shows it is avoidable, having opened Q-0108 and Q-0109 at its own close. **Q-0109 was absorbed into Q-0074** and is `abandoned` after one day, on a measurement neither ticket had: `safe()` is declared byte-for-byte **twice**, in `fanout/fanout.ts:206–208` and `git/git.ts:19–21`, with 23 call sites between them, and both tickets were asking what a caller does with its `null`. Q-0074 keeps the id because a landed decision entry cites it by name. Its first task is now a census of those 23 rather than a widening, and its split seam is written into the body in advance instead of left for a gate to find. **The fifteen pre-cutover ticket bodies were corrected** in the same session: all of them named the deleted `spike/` and nine imposed a *both trees together* rule with no second tree, so each gained an *After the cutover* section saying what is void and where its subject now is — additive only, no verdict moved. ./backlog/Q-0048-core-fanout/requirements/candidate-codex.md:47:14. **Branch reset without a worktree.** When the derived worktree directory does not exist, `resetBranchTo` force-moves the local branch to `sha` from the repository and does not create a worktree. It preserves the known filesystem-only decision: it adds no check for a stale git worktree registration and no repair or pruning. A directory deleted by hand may therefore continue to leave the branch wedged; this preserved defect is reported in the implementation report and cited in source with `Why: preserved defect, see Q-0048 AC-14.`
./backlog/Q-0048-core-fanout/requirements/candidate-codex.md:55:18. **Failed merge.** When the merge command fails, `mergeInto` collects unresolved paths with git’s `U` diff filter, attempts `git merge --abort`, and returns an object with `ok: false`, the conflict paths, and an `error` string limited to the final 500 characters of git stderr or the error message. It does not throw the merge failure, leave a deliberate partial merge in place, resolve conflicts, or silently report success.
./backlog/Q-0048-core-fanout/requirements/candidate-codex.md:59:20. **Bounded command failure and timeout.** A non-zero command returns its status, concatenated stdout followed by stderr, and `timedOut: false`. A killed command, `SIGKILL`, or `ETIMEDOUT` is reported with `timedOut: true` and includes the effective `timeoutMs`; a missing status becomes code `1`. Supplying `options.timeoutMs` overrides the 15-minute default, preserving the value Q-0053 will obtain from `commands.timeout_ms`. A timeout is never converted into an ordinary expected test failure or retried by this module.
./backlog/Q-0048-core-fanout/requirements/candidate-codex.md:65:23. **Preserved validation hazard.** The port adds no branch-name or task-id validation, normalization, escaping, or option guard. In particular, `taskVars` continues to expose an agent-authored `task.id`, while branch interpolation remains outside this ticket. This preserves the latent option-injection hazard reported by Q-0042: argv prevents shell injection but a raw git argument beginning with `-` can still be parsed as an option. Current callers keep it latent by prefixing names with `harness/<ticket-id>/`. The implementation report must name this preserved hazard, and the source must cite it with `Why: preserved defect, see Q-0048 AC-23.` Its absence is not a review blocker and fixing it under Q-0048 is a charter violation.

./backlog/Q-0048-core-fanout/requirements/merged.md:181:Merges `branch` into the branch checked out at `dir` with `git -c user.email=harness@local -c user.name=harness merge --no-ff --no-edit <branch>`. On success returns `{ok: true, conflicts: []}`. On failure it collects `git diff --name-only --diff-filter=U`, runs `git merge --abort` (tolerantly), and returns `{ok: false, conflicts: [...], error}` where `error` is `String(e.stderr ?? e.message).slice(-500)` — the **last** 500 characters, because git's own reason is at the end. It does not throw the merge failure, leave a deliberate partial merge, resolve conflicts, or report success.

./backlog/Q-0048-core-fanout/requirements/merged.md:228:1. **Q-0042 finding 4 — option injection through a branch name.** `ensureWorktree` interpolates what it is handed into `refs/heads/${branch}` and passes it to `worktree add -b <branch>`; argv stops *shell* injection, not *option* injection, since git reads a leading `-` as a flag. `taskVars` is what lifts an agent-authored `task.id` into the namespace those names are built from, and it is preserved unguarded — current callers keep the hazard latent by prefixing `harness/<ticket-id>/`. ./backlog/Q-0048-core-fanout/requirements/merged.md:292:1. **Where does `runCommand`'s 1 MiB `maxBuffer` go?** · Owner: Ruud, at the requirements gate. ./docs/decisions/041-containment-is-derived-from-git-on-each-board-invocation.md:6:1. **The state is selected from git's own exit codes and from nothing else.** `git merge-base --is-ancestor <branch> <base>` exits 0 → contained; exits 1 → provably not contained; any other exit → indeterminate (git failed). Exit 1 is never inferred from a failure, a timeout or an absent binary — conflating "provably not" with "could not answer" manufactures exactly the confident falsehood this ticket removes.
./docs/decisions/041-containment-is-derived-from-git-on-each-board-invocation.md:7:2. **The shallow asymmetry.** In a shallow repository (`git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` reports `true`) an exit 0 still reports contained, because ancestry found in the history that is present is real; an exit 1 becomes indeterminate (shallow clone) with no ahead count, because history that is absent cannot disprove ancestry. A shallow clone does not fail — it answers from the history it has — so the implementation must ask rather than wait for an error.

./backlog/Q-0108-claude-md-and-docs-readme-carry-the-same/review/hand-review-1.txt:642:Since Q-0099 it dispatches eight commands as well as its help — `lint` and `validate` from Q-0091, `runs` from Q-0092, `init` and `ticket` from Q-0093, which are the first two that write, `run` from Q-0094, which is the one that executes, and `board` and `adapters`, which are the two that can only exit 0. That is the whole of the spike's set, so the frame now lists every command it dispatches and dispatches every command it lists. Each is one module named after the command the frame registers, which is what lets `frame.source.test.ts` derive the frame/command split from `COMMANDS` rather than from a list: **a frame module may name none of `core`'s domain helpers, and a command module only the ones its own command needs.** The presentation is the CLI's and the walk is `core`'s, so a lint record reaching a terminal, a browser and a WebSocket carries an escape byte in exactly one of the three.
./backlog/Q-0108-claude-md-and-docs-readme-carry-the-same/review/hand-review-1.txt:748:*Status: v1 plan, 2026-09-07 — **Q-0108 shipped by hand**, the first ticket closed here for the reason that the gate costs more than the work: one clause plus two registrations. Its ruling is that a suite may **read** `CLAUDE.md`, Q-0103 E-2 having made it the human's to **write** and the two being different acts — recorded in the check's own authority comment, with no decision entry owed. **It refuted its own prediction**: the new read did not earn a `turbo-inputs.test.ts` registration, because `pathLiterals` skips any literal with no `/` and so never considers a root-level file at all — twelve of them, invisible to the scanner, covered only by a hand-audited register that fails open for a thirteenth. Reported, not fixed. **Three obligations recorded in prose are now tickets: Q-0110, Q-0111 and Q-0112.** The third is the `owner` default, which `ticket.ts:20` had routed to an unnamed successor since Q-0093 and which **fired twice while the first two were being opened**. Writing it up measured something none of its nine earlier sightings had: `--owner` with no value writes the boolean `true`, and `ticketSchema` declares `owner: z.string()` and refuses it — so `quorum ticket new` can write a ticket the product's own schema rejects, and `quorum board` renders it without noticing. **The two orphaned obligations are tickets: Q-0110 and Q-0111.** Q-0090's GA-4 said *"open the successor"* and nobody did; Q-0100 wrote its question into a `project.ts` JSDoc and into no ticket. Known defects remain visible and are not fixed in passing.
./backlog/Q-0099-cli-board-and-adapters-the-two-commands-/requirements/run-1/candidate-codex.md:44:   The command obtains flows by calling `lintFlowDirectory(path.join(harnessDir, 'flows'))` and retaining records with a `flow`. It does not reproduce the spike’s `readdirSync`, `loadFlow`, and `catch` sequence. Because `lintFlowDirectory` sorts filenames, `chore` supplies the `requirements` hint when the six shipped flows are used, even though `solutioning` consumes the same stage. If `flows/` is absent, the command treats the flow set as empty, renders the board, and exits 0 rather than exposing a raw `ENOENT`.
./backlog/Q-0099-cli-board-and-adapters-the-two-commands-/requirements/run-1/candidate-codex.md:88:   The command processes `claude` and then `codex`, using `getAdapter(name, config.adapters)`. ./backlog/Q-0099-cli-board-and-adapters-the-two-commands-/requirements/run-1/candidate-codex.md:104:   When both checks fail, `quorum adapters` prints both failures, completes the loop, and exits 0. ./backlog/Q-0035-empty-range-diagnostic/requirements/candidate-codex.md:31:2. **The error asserts only verified Git states.** The containment outcome is derived from the command's exit status: exit 0 means `contained`, exit 1 means `not contained`, and any other exit or execution failure means `indeterminate`. The error does not say that a ref was merged, rebased, reset, cherry-picked, or otherwise describe a historical event unless a separate check has established that event. An indeterminate result includes a concise Git failure reason and is never rendered as `not contained`.
./backlog/Q-0097-the-workspace-emits-javascript/dev/chore/run-3/implement-iter-4.md:193:- **The two registered preserved defects** (ground rule 3, and the merged requirement's §9):
./backlog/Q-0042-core-git/requirements/candidate-codex.md:35:9. **Shallow asymmetry.** For an ancestry command that exits 0, `ancestry` returns `contained` regardless of whether the repository is shallow or its shallow state is unknown. For an ancestry command that exits 1: `shallow: false` returns `not-contained`; `shallow: true` returns `indeterminate` with reason `shallow clone`; and `shallow: null` returns `indeterminate` with reason `shallow state unknown` and retains the supplied shallow-probe detail. Neither indeterminate result carries a not-contained claim.
./backlog/Q-0042-core-git/requirements/candidate-codex.md:63:23. **Behaviour-preserving tests.** Unit-level tests covering the ported module move beside `packages/core` and run under the workspace test command. At minimum they independently cover: ancestry exit 0, exit 1, and a non-1 failure; all three shallow values combined with exit 1; exit 0 in a shallow repository; a failed shallow probe; missing and hostile refs; missing base; failed ahead count; empty-range direction and conditional tree comparison; existing and new worktree branches; base fallback to `HEAD`; worktree removal with and without branch deletion; short-SHA failure; and idempotent local exclusion. Tests must assert outcomes rather than depend on the current repository’s branch topology.

./backlog/Q-0091-cli-read-only-commands-board-lint-valida/requirements/run-1/candidate-codex.md:45:13. **CLI — empty board.** `quorum board` exits 0 for an empty backlog and does not print a fatal or indeterminate diagnostic solely because there are no tickets.
./backlog/Q-0091-cli-read-only-commands-board-lint-valida/requirements/run-1/candidate-codex.md:49:15. **CLI — lint exit status.** `quorum lint` exits 0 when the core directory report is successful and exits 1 when it is unsuccessful. The output includes successful file records and all reported diagnostic blocks, including cross-flow and cross-vendor lint failures, without silently dropping records.
./backlog/Q-0091-cli-read-only-commands-board-lint-valida/requirements/run-1/candidate-codex.md:53:17. **CLI — adapters default report.** `quorum adapters` checks the `claude` and `codex` adapters obtained from core using the resolved project’s adapter configuration. For each adapter, it prints either a successful installed-version line or the error returned by `adapter.check()`. One adapter’s failed check does not prevent the other adapter from being checked.
./backlog/Q-0091-cli-read-only-commands-board-lint-valida/requirements/run-1/candidate-codex.md:55:18. **CLI — adapters without probe.** Without `--probe`, the command does not call `probeAdapter`, records successful adapter checks with `login: unverified`, and prints the existing notice that presence alone does not verify subscription login and that `quorum adapters --probe` performs that verification.
./backlog/Q-0091-cli-read-only-commands-board-lint-valida/requirements/run-1/candidate-codex.md:57:19. **CLI — adapters probe.** With `--probe`, each adapter whose check succeeds is passed to core `probeAdapter` with the resolved repository directory. A successful probe prints that login was verified, its round-trip duration, and cost or token fields only when those values are present. A failed probe prints `login not usable` and the core probe error. The probe is the same core operation used to prove subscription readiness; the CLI does not implement a second probe.
./backlog/Q-0091-cli-read-only-commands-board-lint-valida/requirements/run-1/candidate-codex.md:61:21. **CLI — adapters JSON.** With `--json`, `quorum adapters` prints a JSON object after the human-readable report with `probed` set from the presence of `--probe` and an `adapters` array containing one result per checked adapter. Check failures have `installed: false` and the error; successful unprobed checks have `installed: true`, their version, and `login: unverified`; probed checks report `login: verified` or `login: failed` and retain the core probe fields.
./backlog/Q-0091-cli-read-only-commands-board-lint-valida/requirements/run-1/candidate-codex.md:73:27. **CLI — validate aggregation.** `validate` processes all supplied artifacts in argument order. It exits 0 only if every artifact is valid and readable; it exits 1 if one or more artifacts are invalid or unreadable.

./backlog/Q-0034-reconcile-the-unmerged-green-branches/requirements/merged.md:110:**AC-14 — The board says where the code is.** For every ticket at stage `green` or later whose `branch` value resolves to an existing git ref, `harness board` shows `<base>` and one of three factual states: the tip is contained in `<base>`; it is not contained, with how many commits it is ahead; or the state is indeterminate because a required ref is missing. A missing ref is never reported as contained or not contained. The value is derived from git on each invocation and nothing is stored in `ticket.md`. A ticket with no resolvable ref renders as it does today, and the command exits 0 in a repository with no `harness/*` branches at all. Tests cover all three states.

./backlog/Q-0082-the-preflight-reports-what-it-deferred-o/review/hand-review-1.txt:300:Since Q-0099 it dispatches eight commands as well as its help — `lint` and `validate` from Q-0091, `runs` from Q-0092, `init` and `ticket` from Q-0093, which are the first two that write, `run` from Q-0094, which is the one that executes, and `board` and `adapters`, which are the two that can only exit 0. Each is one module named after the command the frame registers, which is what lets `frame.source.test.ts` derive the frame/command split from `COMMANDS` rather than from a list: **a frame module may name none of `core`'s domain helpers, and a command module only the ones its own command needs.** The presentation is the CLI's and the walk is `core`'s, so a lint record reaching a terminal, a browser and a WebSocket carries an escape byte in exactly one of the three.

./backlog/Q-0036-what-green-means-and-where-the-code-is/requirements/candidate-claude.md:48:**AC-1 — The board says where the code is, when it is there.** For an in-scope ticket whose `branch` value names an existing ref, `harness board` renders on that ticket's line the name of `<base>` and one of exactly three states: *contained*, *not contained*, *indeterminate*. When `git merge-base --is-ancestor <branch> <base>` exits 0 the state is *contained*, and no commit count is shown. ./backlog/Q-0036-what-green-means-and-where-the-code-is/requirements/candidate-claude.md:60:**AC-7 — The `branch` value is treated as untrusted input.** `branch` is a field in a file an agent may have written. It reaches git through argv only, never through a shell, and a value that git would otherwise interpret as an option, or that names no ref, produces *indeterminate* rather than being executed. Test: a ticket at `green` whose `branch` is `--upload-pack=touch pwned` renders indeterminate, creates no file named `pwned`, and exits 0. (`spike/src/git.js:7` already carries this reasoning for branch names built from agent-authored task ids; this criterion extends it to the field the board reads.)

./backlog/Q-0105-nothing-checks-whether-main-has-been-val/review/chore/run-2/chore-iter-2.md:7:- major: packages/core/src/git/git.ts:66 Every exit status 128 from the initial work-tree probe is classified as “not a work tree” and ultimately suppressed as `null`. Git also returns 128 when a repository exists but cannot be opened—for example because of dubious ownership or an unsupported repository format, as the preceding JSDoc itself acknowledges. Those are failed probes inside the subject, so silently treating them as absence contradicts AC-3 and the requirement’s rule that an unanswerable check must not report success through silence. Add a classification strategy and regression fixture that distinguish a confirmed non-work-tree from these fatal repository errors without depending on localized stderr; fatal errors that cannot be proven to mean absence must produce `indeterminate (git failed)` while a genuine non-work-tree remains `null`.

./backlog/Q-0036-what-green-means-and-where-the-code-is/requirements/merged.md:73:No state is ever inferred from a ticket's stage, its branch name, its `history`, identical file trees, the working tree, or a remote — two refs with the same tree and no ancestry relationship are *not contained*. Writing it up measured something none of its nine earlier sightings had: `--owner` with no value writes the boolean `true`, and `ticketSchema` declares `owner: z.string()` and refuses it — so `quorum ticket new` can write a ticket the product's own schema rejects, and `quorum board` renders it without noticing. **The two orphaned obligations are tickets: Q-0110 and Q-0111.** Q-0090's GA-4 said *"open the successor"* and nobody did; Q-0100 wrote its question into a `project.ts` JSDoc and into no ticket. They are **two** rather than one, measured rather than merged on the pattern the two same-day merges set: different packages, different decisions, and different forcing functions — a shell script that cannot tell *did the thing* from *did not understand you*, against M3's server surfacing a shell imperative to a browser. **Q-0110 is one wider than GA-4 named**, because a third zero was routed to it later by a source comment, so the obligation grew in the code after the instruction that created it. Creating them reproduced a live preserved defect on the spot: `quorum ticket new` stamped `owner: ruudvanengelenhoven` on both from `process.env.USER` (`backlog.ts:190`), **instances ten and eleven**, corrected by hand as the fourth such correction and still not reaching the code. **Q-0066 was absorbed into Q-0068**, which is retitled and raised to `p2`, and **Q-0109 into Q-0074**. The two merges rest on different evidence and the difference is the point: Q-0074/Q-0109 were literally one function declared twice, while Q-0066/Q-0068 are two unlike defects on **one surface** — `packages/cli/src/adapters.ts`'s own module header names both tickets in one docblock, the combined file set is eleven files that are the same eleven, one gate rules both questions and neither owes a decision entry. **A third defect on that same command has no ticket**: `quorum adapters` exits 0 with both CLIs absent, whose successor is named as *"Q-0090's GA-4"* — an instruction to open a ticket that lives only inside Q-0090's own merged requirement, never acted on, with Q-0090 closed since 2026-09-02. **That is the second orphaned obligation found this week**, after Q-0100's imperative living only in a `project.ts` JSDoc, and together they are a seventh direction of this page's drift: *an obligation recorded inside a closed ticket rather than as a ticket*. Q-0105 is the counter-example that shows it is avoidable, having opened Q-0108 and Q-0109 at its own close. **Q-0109 was absorbed into Q-0074** and is `abandoned` after one day, on a measurement neither ticket had: `safe()` is declared byte-for-byte **twice**, in `fanout/fanout.ts:206–208` and `git/git.ts:19–21`, with 23 call sites between them, and both tickets were asking what a caller does with its `null`. Q-0074 keeps the id because a landed decision entry cites it by name. Its first task is now a census of those 23 rather than a widening, and its split seam is written into the body in advance instead of left for a gate to find. **The fifteen pre-cutover ticket bodies were corrected** in the same session: all of them named the deleted `spike/` and nine imposed a *both trees together* rule with no second tree, so each gained an *After the cutover* section saying what is void and where its subject now is — additive only, no verdict moved. Re-measuring found more than paths: Q-0060's second defect survives the port through a `parsedTasks` that looks like a parse and is a cast, Q-0076's prompt-to-output ratio has halved from 3.4x to 1.97x so its headline no longer aims the fix, and Q-0067 is unblocked with its version drift widened twice in eleven days. **The open list was triaged against the tree and two bullets here were wrong about what is open**: Q-0063 was closed 2026-08-26 and ported by Q-0047 while its bullet still described the defect in the present tense and called CI red for it, and Q-0100 shipped 2026-09-06 while its bullet still read `draft`. Both are corrected below with what shipped. Nothing checks a bullet's stated stage against the ticket's own frontmatter — `plan-backlog.test.ts` asks whether a ticket **exists** on both sides and, under a closed milestone, whether it is still `draft`, and M2 is open — which is a **sixth direction** of the drift this page records five of. The other eighteen open tickets were each checked against the code and every one still has its subject; what has rotted is their **landing constraints**, since all fifteen opened before the cutover name `spike/` and nine impose a *both trees together* landing rule that no longer has a second tree. Q-0100 also closed owing a decision entry recorded only in a `project.ts` JSDoc and in no ticket, which is the Q-0108/Q-0109 shape arriving through a source comment. **Q-0105 is closed**, at `reviewed` and contained, with its two deliberate deferrals opened as **Q-0108** and **Q-0109** rather than left in its entry to expire — which is what an obligation recorded only in a closed ticket's entry does, and this page records five directions of that drift already. **Q-0105 is `reviewed` and contained**, and its GO-3 earned its existence: every local signal was green in both environment rows and CI was red on all three jobs, on a test whose verdict turned out to be a property of the machine — inside the sweep built to forbid exactly that. So the ticket opened because nothing noticed `main` was unvalidated was itself unclosable on a local verification, which is the argument for the obligation made by events rather than by reasoning. 2026-09-06 — **Q-0105 reached `requirements`**, and it caught its own subject while it ran: the body had been corrected hours earlier to say `main` was level with `origin/main`, and the run measured it **2 commits ahead** — that same session's own commits, unnoticed within hours of the zero being recorded. Its iteration 2 is also the third instance of *"a retry on an unchanged tree cannot rule its own blocker"* and the first where the second pass found something anyway, by re-examining whether two of iteration 1's three blockers were blockers at all. **Q-0102 was parked at `p2`**, the first ticket here retired by measuring its own premise dead rather than by shipping anything: 0 failures in 36 local sweeps and 0 in 28 CI sweep cells across fourteen consecutive green runs, against an opening claim that *"every push is red or lucky"* which was an inference made while `main` stood 89 commits ahead of `origin/main` — Q-0105's subject, and now the second thing that gap hid. Its GO-1 is what parked it: at that rate no fix can be shown red before green, and no step in the requirements flow can produce a failure rate, so a run would have been the sixteenth appearance of a loop handed work no agent in it can perform — **recognised before the money was spent, for the first time**. The sighting stands, the reopening threshold is written into the ticket, and the `p1` half that survives is that a flaky oracle trains the reader to re-run until green. **Q-0103 ran the cutover**, so M2's done-when reads one required suite rather than two and Q-0009's *"what is not done"* is discharged: `spike/` is deleted with its CI job, the three port-freeze jobs, `harness/port-charter.md` and `packages/core/src/spike-parity.test.ts`, and Q-0010's second inherited obligation — the binary half of that suite — is recorded as landed in Q-0095 and Q-0101. The transfer share this page moved four times went with the register that re-derived it, having measured how much work was left rather than a property of the workspace. ./backlog/Q-0082-the-preflight-reports-what-it-deferred-o/review/hand-review-3.txt:2340:backlog/Q-0036-what-green-means-and-where-the-code-is/requirements/merged.md:73:No state is ever inferred from a ticket's stage, its branch name, its `history`, identical file trees, the working tree, or a remote — two refs with the same tree and no ancestry relationship are *not contained*. The board performs no `fetch`, `pull`, `ls-remote` or any other subcommand that contacts a remote, does not deepen a shallow clone, and behaves identically offline. Every git invocation passes an argv array to `execFileSync`, never a shell string, and treats `t.meta.branch` as untrusted input disambiguated as `refs/heads/<branch>`: a ticket whose `branch` is `--upload-pack=touch pwned` renders unannotated or indeterminate, creates no file named `pwned`, and exits 0. The containment helper lives in `spike/src/git.js` beside the existing argv rule, not inline in `spike/bin/harness.js`, so `materialiseDiff`'s duplicate closure has one place to move in M2's port.

./backlog/Q-0105-nothing-checks-whether-main-has-been-val/runs.log:23:2026-09-07T06:15:00.000Z post-merge fix (out-of-band: CI run 34089259734 was RED on all three jobs at cbf45fc, deterministically, on one test — git.test.ts's `a repository git refuses to open for OWNERSHIP is `git failed` too`. It stages dubious ownership with GIT_TEST_ASSUME_DIFFERENT_OWNER, git's own hook, which is honoured by git 2.55.0 on darwin and NOT by git 2.55.0 on ubuntu-latest — the same version string either side. So the test's verdict was a property of the machine, which "A test's verdict is a property of the commit, not of the checkout or the account" (2026-08-30) forbids, and it was red inside the sweep built to enforce that rule: the third instance this week of a check being an instance of its own subject. The implementer's premise assertion is what caught it rather than letting it pass vacuously, and its comment had anticipated exactly this — "what keeps this honest if a future git drops it, since the failure then names the hook rather than the code". It named the hook. What was wrong is that a test needing an environment capability was written unconditional. Fixed by hand after the gate on Q-0073's and Q-0080's precedent: the premise is now a probe, and where the hook is absent the test REPORTS A SKIP naming what could not be staged, which is the sanctioned shape rather than a silent pass. Demonstrated both ways rather than read — hook present 79 passed, hook simulated absent 78 passed | 1 SKIPPED, so the skip is reported and never counted as success. The coverage does not move: the repository-format case at :705 reaches the same `repositoryAt` -> `git failed` path, stages itself by writing a file, needs no capability and runs everywhere, so what a skip costs is a fixture and not the assertion. The mechanism behind Ubuntu's git not honouring the hook is NOT identified and is deliberately not guessed at in the source. Local verification is unchanged and was never wrong — 21/21 forced 0 cached and the sweep exit 0 both before and after — which is the whole of GO-3's argument: this ticket could not have been closed on a local verification.)

# Engineering rules

- TypeScript strict; no `any`, no `@ts-ignore` without a one-line reason on the same line.
- No deprecated API in new code; one found in code you are already changing is reported, not migrated in passing — the migration is its own change. `tsc --noEmit` does not error on `@deprecated`; it is an editor strikethrough. Since Q-0069 `pnpm lint` does: `@typescript-eslint/no-deprecated` is on at error severity, with the type information it needs, and it is the only type-aware rule enabled. It covers exactly what ESLint covers — `packages/**/*.ts` and `apps/**/*.ts`, tests included.
- Prefer small, boring, proven libraries. A new dependency needs a one-line justification in the PR and, if it changes architecture, a DECISIONS.md entry.
- Every behaviour change ships with a test. Core logic is tested through the mock adapter end-to-end suite plus focused unit tests; adapters are tested by the local `adapters --probe` and its JSON report.
- Files are the database. Anything persistent is a file in `backlog/`, `harness/`, or `.quorum/`; no hidden state in the daemon.
- One trace/event format (`packages/shared`). Adapters map to it; nothing downstream knows which vendor produced an event.
- Errors are explicit: invalid structured output saves the raw text next to the ticket and stops the run with a clear message. Never default silently.
- Safety by construction: worktrees, integration branch, `human-locked` gate on deploy — enforced in `core`, not in the UI or by convention. Budget caps are specified but not yet enforced; do not rely on one stopping a run.
- Conventional commits, ticket id in the subject (`feat(core): bounded backward edges [Q-0009]`).
- Comments are JSDoc (`/** … */`) on modules, exported symbols and non-obvious fields; `//` only for a short remark inside a body. Code that needs explaining is written wrong — fix the name or the shape first. Where behaviour is deliberately counterintuitive, one line naming the authority (`Why: preserved defect, see Q-0043 AC-7`); never transcribe DECISIONS.md or a ticket body into a source file.
- Keep the cold-clone test in mind: if a feature makes the first 30 minutes longer, it needs a reason.
# Product boundaries

- Quorum is product-agnostic. Nothing in this repository references feedmind, flextann or any other SaaS product except as an example name in demo data, or as named dogfood targets in positioning prose (01-product-definition.md and the development plan, per the 2026-08-22 decision). Product knowledge belongs in *that* repo's `harness/` context files.
- "Harness" is the concept and the folder (`harness/`); "Quorum" is the product. Never call the product a harness, never call the folder quorum.
- BYOS — bring your own subscriptions. No API keys, ever, on any code path, including tests and docs examples.
- The vendor CLIs keep owning interactive hacking. Quorum owns the flow; it does not rebuild a chat IDE.
- Flows are YAML files in the project; the UI edits files and never holds the truth.
- Human-gated by default; `auto` is opt-in per gate; `human-locked` cannot be overridden.
- Out of v1 (do not build by accident): multi-user, remote daemon, cloud sync, plugin marketplace, visual node canvas, eval suites, Gemini adapter (roadmap, community), desktop shell.
# Docs and decisions

- The decisions are append-only. Each is one file in `docs/decisions/`, named `NNN-slug.md`, opening `# <title> — <YYYY-MM-DD>` and carrying **Decision**, **Alternatives considered**, **Why**; `docs/DECISIONS.md` is the index and gains one line per entry. A landed entry is never edited — reversing one is a new entry that names the old one. Cite an entry by its title and date, never by its file name or number.
- **An entry's date is the date it takes its place in the index**, not the date the choice was made; where the two differ the body says when it was decided. `packages/shared/src/docs.test.ts` enforces it — the index's dates never go backwards — so a back-dated entry turns the suite red. See *"An entry's date is the date it takes its place in the index"* (2026-09-01).
- `docs/GLOSSARY.md` is the vocabulary. When you need a new term, add it there before using it in a second file. Do not introduce synonyms for existing terms.
- The numbered docs (01–06) are living documents; edit in place and bump the status line at the top with the date and what changed.
- When code and docs disagree, the docs are wrong until a DECISIONS.md entry says otherwise — fix the docs in the same PR.
- Milestone ends with a DECISIONS.md entry: what was learned, what changed.
- Write prose, not bullet walls, in the numbered docs; bullets are fine in rules and plans.
     1	# What an exit code may claim, and the three zeros it was asked about — 2026-09-08
     2	
     3	**Decision:** an exit code reports **what became of the command**, and `SUCCESS` is reserved for a
     4	command that did what it was asked. Q-0110 inherited three zeros from Q-0090's gate obligation GA-4
     5	and asked which of the five codes each should take. **Two of the three are correct as they stand and
     6	are ratified rather than repaired; one is wrong and changes.** No sixth code is invented — the
     7	`ExitCode` union is closed at 0, 1, 2, 3, 130, and this entry does not widen it.
     8	
     9	**(a) An unknown or absent command exits `ERROR`, and `help` goes on exiting `SUCCESS`.** This is
    10	the one that changes. `SUCCESS`'s own definition is *"The command did what it was asked"*: a name
    11	this binary does not dispatch was not done, and a bare invocation asked for nothing. Both print the
    12	help, both then exit 1, and the line between them and `quorum help` is **help on purpose is success;
    13	help by accident is not**. The concrete failure is Q-0090's own, in its words — *"a shell script
    14	cannot tell 'did the thing' from 'did not understand you'"* — and a typo in a pipeline is the case
    15	that costs something, because it passes silently today.
    16	
    17	**(b) `regressed` goes on exiting `SUCCESS`, ruled rather than fallen-through.** Q-0090 registered
    18	this as a defect because nobody had decided it: `spike/bin/harness.js:557` named only `aborted` and
    19	`undecided`, so `regressed` reached a fallthrough. It was right that no one had chosen and wrong
    20	that the value was wrong.
    21	
    22	**An exit code here reports a run's disposition, not its verdict.** `regressed` is what a cross-flow
    23	backward edge produces (`engine.ts:336`): the flow ran to a terminal state, the ticket's stage moved
    24	to the target flow's `consumes`, and the branch it proved was kept. `core` already sides it with
    25	`completed` in **every** question it asks of a terminal status. `lifecycle.ts` carries three
    26	predicates where one named `finished` used to be — its own comment says so — and `regressed` answers
    27	each of them the way `completed` does: `advancesStage` (`:22`) and `returnsWorktrees` (`:30`) both
    28	read `completed || regressed`, and `restoresBranch` (`:40`) is `aborted || failed || interrupted`,
    29	so a regressed run is not rolled back. `02-sdlc-pipeline-spec.md` says the same from the other side
    30	— *"only `completed` and `regressed` move the stage"*. A CLI that exited non-zero for one of the two stage-moving statuses would put the exit
    31	table out of step with the engine's own model of what a finished run is, in a file whose stated
    32	purpose is that *"the meanings are owned here rather than re-decided by each command"*.
    33	
    34	The cost is stated rather than hidden: a caller cannot tell an approved review from a regressed one
    35	by the exit code, and must read the terminal event, which carries the status by name. That is what
    36	the event stream is for, and a review returning `revise` is the flow working rather than failing —
    37	teaching a reader that a normal outcome prints a failure is how an oracle stops being read at all.
    38	
    39	**(c) `quorum adapters` goes on exiting `SUCCESS` with nothing installed; `--probe` exits `ERROR`
    40	when a login is not usable.** One command, two contracts, because it answers two questions — the
    41	shape `git diff` and `git diff --exit-code` already have.
    42	
    43	The product says which is which, in the command's own output: without `--probe` it prints
    44	*"presence only — logins NOT verified; run `quorum adapters --probe` before a real run"*. A command
    45	that disclaims being the gate is a **report**, and this CLI's reports — `board`, `runs` — exit 0
    46	over an empty backlog and an empty run store alike. `--probe` is the **check**, and this CLI's
    47	checks — `lint`, `validate` — exit 1 when the answer is no. *"check() proves presence; only
    48	`adapters --probe` proves login"* (2026-08-22) drew that line before either command existed; this
    49	entry gives it an exit code.
    50	
    51	So the zero Q-0099 preserved at AC-8(c) was the *presence* path and is correct. What was never
    52	decided is the probe's, and a probe that reports an unusable login while exiting 0 is the failure
    53	that decision exists to prevent — two green ticks followed by a vendor stack trace, one command
    54	earlier.
    55	
    56	**Alternatives considered.** *`regressed` → `ERROR`* — the reading Q-0110's body assumed, and the
    57	one a pipeline argument supports: `quorum run review X && quorum run qa-final X` would chain past a
    58	regression. Rejected because the second command refuses on its stage precondition anyway, so the
    59	damage is contained, and because `failed` already holds 1 — collapsing the two would tell a caller
    60	that a run which broke and a run which did its job are the same thing. *A sixth code for
    61	"finished, verdict negative"* — rejected: `ExitCode` is closed deliberately so a sixth cannot be
    62	written by accident, and widening it for one status trades a real guarantee for a distinction the
    63	terminal event already carries. *Bare `adapters` → `ERROR` with nothing installed* — rejected on the
    64	command's own disclaimer, and because it would make the only machine-readable inventory of what is
    65	installed unusable from a script that has to tolerate a missing vendor.
    66	
    67	**Why.** GA-4 said *"open the successor for the exit table's two zeros"* and no ticket was ever
    68	created; Q-0110 was opened 2026-09-07 when a triage found it, and it is one zero wider than GA-4
    69	named because Q-0099 routed a third to it from a source comment. Deciding the three together is the
    70	point — each was preserved by a different ticket for a different reason, and none of those tickets
    71	was in a position to say what 0 means. This entry says it once so the next command inherits an
    72	answer rather than a fallthrough.
     1	---
     2	id: Q-0110
     3	title: The CLI reports success three times over for a non-success
     4	stage: draft
     5	owner: ruud
     6	repos: []
     7	branch: harness/Q-0110/integration
     8	priority: p2
     9	created: 2026-09-07
    10	iterations: {}
    11	history: []
    12	---
    13	Three exit paths return 0 where nothing succeeded. Each is a registered preserved defect, each
    14	names this ticket's origin in its own authority line, and the decision they share — **what may an
    15	exit code claim** — has never been taken. Decide it once, then close all three together.
    16	
    17	**Opened 2026-09-07, and the reason it is being opened now is the finding.** Q-0090's gate
    18	obligation **GA-4** says, in as many words, *"open the successor for the exit table's two zeros"*.
    19	Searched on 2026-09-07, `GA-4` appears only inside `backlog/Q-0090-…/requirements/merged.md`. No
    20	ticket was ever created, and Q-0090 closed on 2026-09-02. That is an obligation recorded inside a
    21	closed ticket rather than as a ticket — the shape this project has now recorded twice in one week,
    22	the other being Q-0100's, and the shape Q-0105 avoided by opening Q-0108 and Q-0109 at its own
    23	close.
    24	
    25	## The three zeros, measured 2026-09-07
    26	
    27	| # | site | what exits 0 |
    28	| --- | --- | --- |
    29	| 1 | `packages/cli/src/main.ts:85–92` | an **unknown or absent command** prints the help and returns |
    30	| 2 | `packages/cli/src/exit.ts:61–63` | **`regressed`** shares `completed`'s `SUCCESS` |
    31	| 3 | `packages/cli/src/adapters.ts:20–23` | **`quorum adapters` with both CLIs absent** |
    32	
    33	Each carries a `Why: preserved defect` line naming its authority — Q-0090 AC-6, Q-0090 AC-4(c) and
    34	Q-0099 AC-8(c) — and each routes here. `main.ts:83` says *"The successor is Q-0090's GA-4"*;
    35	`exit.ts:62–63` says *"routed to Q-0090's GA-4 successor together with the unknown-command zero"*;
    36	`adapters.ts:22` says *"the successor is Q-0090's GA-4, which carries the unknown-command zero for
    37	the same reason"*.
    38	
    39	**GA-4 named two of the three.** Its text is *"An unknown or absent command exits 0 (AC-6), and
    40	`regressed` shares `completed`'s fallthrough (AC-4(c))"*. The third arrived later, routed here by a
    41	source comment Q-0099 wrote — so **the obligation grew in the code after the instruction that
    42	created it was written**, and reading GA-4 alone under-scopes this ticket by one. That is worth
    43	saying out loud because it is the same failure mode one layer down: a record that stops being
    44	complete without anyone editing it.
    45	
    46	## Why each is not merely cosmetic
    47	
    48	**(1)** is the one an adopter meets. `spike/bin/harness.js:560–562` was a `default:` branch that
    49	printed usage and returned, and the port preserved it. A shell script or a CI step cannot tell *"did
    50	the thing"* from *"did not understand you"*, so a typo in a pipeline is a silent pass.
    51	
    52	**(2)** is the one a *run* meets. `regressed` is what a backward edge produces — the ticket's stage
    53	moved **backwards** — and the CLI reports it as success. The mechanism that caused it is already
    54	closed: `EXIT_CODE_FOR_STATUS` is keyed `Readonly<Record<RunTerminalEvent['status'], ExitCode>>`, so
    55	a seventh status fails to compile rather than falling through, which is `exit.ts`'s own JSDoc saying
    56	*"which is exactly how `regressed` came to share `completed`'s code"*. **What remains is the value,
    57	not the shape** — and a value is exactly what a compiler cannot check.
    58	
    59	**(3)** is the one a *cold clone* meets. `quorum adapters` on a machine with no vendor CLI installed
    60	reports success, which is the command that exists to de-risk a paid run answering that the risk was
    61	checked.
    62	
    63	## The decision, which is one question with three answers
    64	
    65	**What may an exit code claim?** The vocabulary already exists and is closed —
    66	`SUCCESS` 0, `ERROR` 1, `ABORTED` 2, `UNDECIDED` 3, `SIGNAL` 130 (`exit.ts:15–38`), with `ExitCode`
    67	a union so a sixth cannot be written by accident. So this is not "invent a code"; it is "choose among
    68	five, three times", and the three answers need not be the same:
    69	
    70	- an unmet **precondition** (no CLI installed) is not the same as
    71	- a **misunderstood request** (unknown command), which is not the same as
    72	- a **completed run whose outcome moved the ticket backwards** (`regressed`).
    73	
    74	`UNDECIDED`'s own entry is the precedent for how carefully this is done here: it is *"distinct from
    75	`ERROR` on purpose"* because somebody who supplied a word that is not an answer **was** there. The
    76	same care is owed to each of these three.
    77	
    78	## What it must not do
    79	
    80	**Change one and leave the others.** They are one ticket because they are one question; closing the
    81	loudest and leaving two behind would leave the authority lines pointing at a ticket that is done.
    82	
    83	**Move a code without moving what reads it.** `EXIT_CODE_FOR_STATUS` is read by `run.ts`, and
    84	`exit.test.ts` derives its key set from `@quorum/shared`'s schema rather than transcribing it —
    85	`failure-paths.test.ts:549` is the one assertion in `packages/cli` that reads a **spawned** process's
    86	status for a non-zero code, and it is 3. A change here is a change to a contract that is proven
    87	across a real process boundary in exactly one place.
    88	
    89	**Decide the colour policy.** GA-4 is explicit: *"None is opened for colour policy until a README
    90	exists to say what a stranger sees."* That stays true.
    91	
    92	## Non-goals
    93	
    94	Q-0068's BYOS refusal string and `probeAdapter`'s null dereference, which are that ticket's and sit
    95	on the same command for a different reason; the help text's content; `UNDECIDED`'s and `SIGNAL`'s
    96	values, which are settled; and any new exit code, which the `ExitCode` union forbids by design.
     1	/**
     2	 * The command line, parsed exactly as `spike/bin/harness.js:25–42` parses it.
     3	 *
     4	 * Deliberately not a library. Two of the behaviours below are defects a well-behaved parser would
     5	 * silently fix — a single-dash token is a positional, and `--` swallows the token after it instead
     6	 * of terminating the flags — and fixing either before a single command is ported would change flag
     7	 * semantics under the eight commands that are about to arrive. They are preserved and pinned in
     8	 * `argv.test.ts`, one row each, so a later change to them is a deliberate act. Why: preserved
     9	 * defects, see Q-0090 AC-2 behaviours 4 and 5.
    10	 */
    11	
    12	/** What a flag carries: the token after it, or `true` when the next token is another flag. */
    13	export type FlagValue = string | true;
    14	
    15	/** The flag that accumulates. Every other flag is last-wins. */
    16	export const GATE_ANSWER = 'gate-answer';
    17	
    18	/** One command line, split into the four things the commands read. */
    19	export interface ParsedArgv {
    20	  /** The first positional, or `undefined` when there is none. */
    21	  readonly cmd: string | undefined;
    22	  /** Every positional after the first, in order, with nothing de-duplicated. */
    23	  readonly rest: readonly string[];
    24	  /**
    25	   * Every flag seen. `gate-answer` holds an array; every other key holds its last value.
    26	   *
    27	   * Nothing is coerced to a number: a value is the token as it was typed, or the boolean `true`.
    28	   */
    29	  readonly flags: Readonly<Record<string, FlagValue | FlagValue[]>>;
    30	  /**
    31	   * Every `--gate-answer` in command-line order, as a copy taken at parse time.
    32	   *
    33	   * A non-interactive run may cross several gates in one invocation and each needs its own answer,
    34	   * which is why this one flag accumulates where every other is last-wins. See Q-0033.
    35	   */
    36	  readonly gateAnswers: readonly FlagValue[];
    37	}
    38	
    39	/**
    40	 * Split `argv` — already sliced past the node executable and the script — into {@link ParsedArgv}.
    41	 *
    42	 * There is no parse error, because the spike has none: an unrecognised shape becomes a flag or a
    43	 * positional, and the command decides what to do with it. Inventing one here would be a behaviour
    44	 * change, not a port.
    45	 */
    46	export function parseArgv(argv: readonly string[]): ParsedArgv {
    47	  const flags: Record<string, FlagValue | FlagValue[]> = {};
    48	  const positional: string[] = [];
    49	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
    50	    const token = argv[i];
    51	    if (token.startsWith('--')) {
    52	      const key = token.slice(2);
    53	      const next = argv[i + 1];
    54	      const value: FlagValue = next && !next.startsWith('--') ? argv[++i] : true;
    55	      if (key === GATE_ANSWER) {
    56	        const seen = flags[key];
    57	        flags[key] = [...(Array.isArray(seen) ? seen : []), value];
    58	      } else {
    59	        flags[key] = value;
    60	      }
    61	    } else {
    62	      positional.push(token);
    63	    }
    64	  }
    65	  const [cmd, ...rest] = positional;
    66	  const answers = flags[GATE_ANSWER];
    67	  return { cmd, rest, flags, gateAnswers: Array.isArray(answers) ? [...answers] : [] };
    68	}
     1	/**
     2	 * The two ways this CLI reports a failure, kept apart because they are two mechanisms and not one
     3	 * number spelled twice.
     4	 *
     5	 * {@link die} exits immediately, which truncates whatever the process had not finished writing.
     6	 * {@link failSoftly} sets the status and lets the process finish writing, which is what the spike's
     7	 * four `process.exitCode = 1` assignments on the `runs` paths rely on
     8	 * (`spike/bin/harness.js:499`, `:517`, `:523`, `:531`): a listing that reports a store warning
     9	 * still prints the listing. A port collapsing the two into one call loses that output — see
    10	 * Q-0090 AC-5, whose test demonstrates the difference rather than asserting it.
    11	 */
    12	import { c } from './colour.js';
    13	import { ERROR } from './exit.js';
    14	
    15	/**
    16	 * Print a message to stderr and stop the process immediately with {@link ERROR}.
    17	 *
    18	 * Preserved verbatim from `spike/bin/harness.js:124`, including the space *inside* the red span,
    19	 * which every other call site in that file puts outside it. Why: preserved defect, see Q-0090 AC-3.
    20	 */
    21	export function die(message: string): never {
    22	  console.error(c.red('✗ ') + message);
    23	  process.exit(ERROR);
    24	}
    25	
    26	/**
    27	 * Fail because no project was found, appending the remedy this surface offers for it.
    28	 *
    29	 * The one place that imperative exists; six commands call it and none composes anything. It takes
    30	 * the condition as a string rather than the error, so this frame module names no `core` symbol.
    31	 *
    32	 * Why: see *"A `core` error names the condition; the remedy belongs to the surface"* (2026-09-07).
    33	 */
    34	export function dieNoProject(condition: string): never {
    35	  return die(`${condition} — run \`quorum init\` in your repo`);
    36	}
    37	
    38	/**
    39	 * Record that the command failed without stopping it, so pending output still reaches the terminal.
    40	 *
    41	 * The counterpart to {@link die}, and never a synonym for it.
    42	 */
    43	export function failSoftly(): void {
    44	  process.exitCode = ERROR;
    45	}
    46	
    47	/**
    48	 * The handler an unexpected throw reaches, so a crash prints a Node stack through the error path.
    49	 *
    50	 * `spike/bin/harness.js:569` is `main().catch((e) => die(e.stack ?? String(e)))`, and four of its
    51	 * behaviours belong to that exact expression rather than to the idea behind it, each measured
    52	 * against it: the property access is unguarded, so a thrown `null` or `undefined` **raises** before
    53	 * `die` is reached; the `??` tests whether `stack` is *there* and never what type it is, so
    54	 * `{ stack: 42 }` is reported as `42`; the fallback is `String(e)`, so a thrown symbol prints as
    55	 * `Symbol(s)`; and a symbol-valued `stack` reaches `die`, where the `+` **raises** because it cannot
    56	 * coerce one. The expression is therefore written out rather than paraphrased, and the coercion is
    57	 * left where the spike leaves it — inside {@link die}. Why: preserved, see Q-0090 AC-3.
    58	 *
    59	 * The two raising rows are a defect and are reported rather than repaired here: the one path that
    60	 * exists to turn a crash into a message replaces it with a different crash. Ground rule 3.
    61	 *
    62	 * The binary that wires this to the frame's entry does not exist yet and is Q-0096's; the behaviour
    63	 * is declared and tested here so no sibling command ticket has to invent it.
    64	 */
    65	export function dieOnUnexpected(error: unknown): never {
    66	  const message: unknown = (error as { stack?: unknown }).stack ?? String(error);
    67	  // The assertion is the point rather than a convenience: `die` takes a string, and this is the one
    68	  // call site that may hand it something else, so the `+` inside `die` performs the same coercion —
    69	  // and raises the same TypeError on a symbol — that the spike's does.
    70	  return die(message as string);
    71	}
     1	/**
     2	 * `quorum adapters [--probe] [--json]` — which vendor CLIs Quorum can see, and whether their logins
     3	 * still answer. The command an adopter is told to run first, and the one that de-risks a paid run
     4	 * before it is paid for.
     5	 *
     6	 * **Presence and login are two questions and this module never lets the cheap one stand in for the
     7	 * expensive one.** `check()` proves the binary runs, and refuses outright where the environment says
     8	 * BYOS is not being honoured; only `probeAdapter` makes an authenticated request. Without `--probe`
     9	 * every success
    10	 * is recorded `login: 'unverified'` and the notice below says so — see *"check() proves presence;
    11	 * only `adapters --probe` proves login"* (2026-08-22).
    12	 *
    13	 * **Nothing here decides what a refusal says.** The BYOS refusal is each vendor adapter's own, in
    14	 * `@quorum/core`, and this module renders `e.message` unaltered; the sentence it still carries calls
    15	 * the *product* a harness, which is Q-0068's and is preserved verbatim (Q-0099 AC-8(a)). The notice
    16	 * below names the *binary*, and that class was ruled by Q-0100: it is `quorum`.
    17	 *
    18	 * **This command answers two questions and reports a status for one of them.** Without `--probe` it
    19	 * is a report and exits 0 whatever it finds, including a machine with no vendor CLI at all — its
    20	 * own last line disclaims being the gate. With `--probe` it is a check, and a login that is not
    21	 * usable is ERROR. Why: see *"What an exit code may claim, and the three zeros it was asked about"*
    22	 * (2026-09-08), which ratified the first and changed the second.
    23	 *
    24	 * **One preserved defect reaches this command and is not repaired here** (ground rule 3):
    25	 *
    26	 * `probeAdapter` dereferences a null `usage`, so an adapter whose login is perfect and which
    27	 *    reports no measure answers `✗ login not usable: Cannot read properties of null`. Why: preserved
    28	 *    defect, see Q-0066, which lands in both trees together — a fix here would leave the spike
    29	 *    disagreeing with `core` until the cutover.
    30	 *
    31	 * Why: behaviour preserved from `spike/bin/harness.js:406–424` (Q-0099 AC-7).
    32	 */
    33	import { getAdapter, loadProject, probeAdapter, ProjectNotFoundError } from '@quorum/core';
    34	
    35	import type { FlagValue } from './argv.js';
    36	import { c } from './colour.js';
    37	import { dieNoProject, failSoftly } from './fail.js';
    38	import type { CommandHandler } from './main.js';
    39	
    40	/**
    41	 * The project whose `adapters` configuration and directory this command uses, or the spike's
    42	 * sentence and a hard exit where none is there.
    43	 *
    44	 * Why: a sixth copy of `lint.ts`'s block rather than a shared helper, because a frame module naming
    45	 * `loadProject` is what `frame.source.test.ts`'s AC-10 partition forbids (Q-0099 AC-3, OQ-5).
    46	 * The message is `core`'s, rendered unaltered — this module composes no recovery advice of its own,
    47	 * which is what keeps one sentence in one place. `--project` is passed through per Q-0091 erratum
    48	 * E-6.
    49	 */
    50	function projectOf(project: FlagValue | readonly FlagValue[] | undefined): ReturnType<typeof loadProject> {
    51	  try {
    52	    return loadProject(project as string | undefined);
    53	  } catch (error) {
    54	    if (!(error instanceof ProjectNotFoundError)) throw error;
    55	    return dieNoProject(error.message);
    56	  }
    57	}
    58	
    59	/**
    60	 * The two vendors, in the order the spike reports them.
    61	 *
    62	 * `mock` is deliberately absent: it is an adapter a flow may select and not a subscription anybody
    63	 * has, so reporting it would say a login is fine that nobody logged into.
    64	 */
    65	const VENDORS = ['claude', 'codex'];
    66	
    67	/**
    68	 * The machine-readable report, one entry per vendor, in the key order the spike writes it.
    69	 *
    70	 * `Record<string, unknown>` rather than a union, because the probe's own result is **spread last**
    71	 * and `--json`'s shape is that object as it stands: a declared shape here would be a second
    72	 * description of `ProbeResult` that could drift from it. Preserved key for key, spread included
    73	 * (Q-0099 AC-7(5)).
    74	 */
    75	type Report = Record<string, unknown>[];
    76	
    77	/** Report which vendor CLIs are installed, and with `--probe` whether each login answers. */
    78	export const adapters: CommandHandler = async ({ flags }) => {
    79	  const { config, repoDir } = projectOf(flags.project);
    80	  // Both read as truthiness, which is what `argv.ts` leaves them as: a flag given no value is the
    81	  // boolean `true` and one given a value is that token, so `--probe` and `--probe x` behave alike.
    82	  const probe = Boolean(flags.probe);
    83	  const asJson = Boolean(flags.json);
    84	  const report: Report = [];
    85	  for (const name of VENDORS) {
    86	    const adapter = getAdapter(name, config.adapters);
    87	    let version: string;
    88	    try {
    89	      version = await adapter.check();
    90	      console.log(`${c.green('✓')} ${name}: ${version}`);
    91	    } catch (error) {
    92	      // The loop continues: one absent CLI is not a reason to stop reporting the other, which is
    93	      // the whole of what an adopter is running this to find out. The message is the adapter's own,
    94	      // rendered unaltered — including the BYOS refusal, which is Q-0068's sentence and not this
    95	      // module's to rewrite on the way through.
    96	      console.log(`${c.red('✗')} ${name}: ${(error as Error).message}`);
    97	      report.push({ adapter: name, installed: false, error: (error as Error).message });
    98	      continue;
    99	    }
   100	
   101	    if (!probe) {
   102	      report.push({ adapter: name, installed: true, version, login: 'unverified' });
   103	      continue;
   104	    }
   105	    // check() only proves the binary exists. Only a real request proves the subscription answers.
   106	    const result = await probeAdapter(adapter, { cwd: repoDir });
   107	    if (result.ok) {
   108	      const cost = result.cost_usd != null ? `, $${result.cost_usd.toFixed(4)}` : '';
   109	      const tokens = result.tokens ? `, ${String(result.tokens)} tokens` : '';
   110	      console.log(`  ${c.green('✓')}${c.dim(` login verified — round-trip ${String(result.ms)}ms${cost}${tokens}`)}`);
   111	    } else {
   112	      console.log(`  ${c.red('✗')} ${c.bold('login not usable')}: ${result.error}`);
   113	    }
   114	    report.push({ adapter: name, installed: true, version, login: result.ok ? 'verified' : 'failed', ...result });
   115	  }
   116	  if (!probe) console.log(c.dim('· presence only — logins NOT verified; run `quorum adapters --probe` before a real run'));
   117	  // After the human lines rather than instead of them: `--json` is a combined stream in the spike
   118	  // and a consumer piping it gets both. Redefining it as JSON-only is a contract change.
   119	  if (asJson) console.log(JSON.stringify({ probed: probe, adapters: report }, null, 2));
   120	  // `--probe` is the check and the presence listing is the report, so only the first answers with a
   121	  // status. Set rather than thrown, so the listing and the `--json` above still reach the terminal.
   122	  // Why: see *"What an exit code may claim, and the three zeros it was asked about"* (2026-09-08).
   123	  if (probe && report.some((entry) => entry.login !== 'verified')) failSoftly();
   124	};
    65	    console.log(HELP);
    66	  },
    67	  init,
    68	  ticket,
    69	  board,
    70	  run,
    71	  lint,
    72	  adapters,
    73	  validate,
    74	  runs,
    75	};
    76	
    77	/**
    78	 * Run one command line. `argv` is `process.argv.slice(2)` when a binary calls it, and a literal
    79	 * array when a test does — the parser reads nothing from the environment, so the two are the same.
    80	 *
    81	 * An unknown or absent command prints the help and reports {@link ERROR}; asking for help — the
    82	 * `help` command, `--help` or `-h` — prints the same text and reports success. Help on purpose is
    83	 * success; help by accident is not.
    84	 *
    85	 * The status is **set rather than exited**, so `main` still resolves and the help still reaches the
    86	 * terminal: `die` and `failSoftly` are two mechanisms and this is the second (Q-0090 AC-5).
    87	 *
    88	 * Why: see *"What an exit code may claim, and the three zeros it was asked about"* (2026-09-08),
    89	 * which changed this one and ratified the two beside it.
    90	 */
    91	/** The two ways to ask for help without naming the command. */
    92	const HELP_TOKENS = new Set(['--help', '-h']);
    93	
    94	/**
    95	 * Whether this invocation asked for the help it is about to get, rather than tripping into it.
    96	 *
    97	 * **Help must be the whole request.** `quorum nonsense --help` named something this binary does not
    98	 * dispatch, so it is ERROR like any other unknown name — a scan of the raw argv would have called it
    99	 * success and contradicted the ruling it implements.
   100	 *
   101	 * `-h` is read as a positional because `parseArgv` recognises only `--` forms (`argv.ts:53`), which
   102	 * is also why the flag half is checked separately: `--adapter -h` consumes `-h` as that flag's
   103	 * value, and no help was asked for there.
   104	 */
   105	const askedForHelp = ({ cmd, rest, flags }: ParsedArgv): boolean => {
   106	  const positionals = cmd === undefined ? rest : [cmd, ...rest];
   107	  if (!positionals.every((token) => HELP_TOKENS.has(token))) return false;
   108	  return flags.help === true || positionals.length > 0;
   109	};
   110	
   111	export async function main(argv: readonly string[]): Promise<void> {
   112	  const parsed = parseArgv(argv);
   113	  const { cmd } = parsed;
   114	  if (cmd !== undefined && isCommand(cmd)) {
   115	    await HANDLERS[cmd](parsed);
   116	    return;
   117	  }
   118	  console.log(HELP);
   119	  if (!askedForHelp(parsed)) failSoftly();
   120	}

Reported, not fixed. **Three obligations recorded in prose are now tickets: Q-0110, Q-0111 and Q-0112.** The third is the `owner` default, which `ticket.ts:20` had routed to an unnamed successor since Q-0093 and which **fired twice while the first two were being opened**. Writing it up measured something none of its nine earlier sightings had: `--owner` with no value writes the boolean `true`, and `ticketSchema` declares `owner: z.string()` and refuses it — so `quorum ticket new` can write a ticket the product's own schema rejects, and `quorum board` renders it without noticing. **The two orphaned obligations are tickets: Q-0110 and Q-0111.** Q-0090's GA-4 said *"open the successor"* and nobody did; Q-0100 wrote its question into a `project.ts` JSDoc and into no ticket. They are **two** rather than one, measured rather than merged on the pattern the two same-day merges set: different packages, different decisions, and different forcing functions — a shell script that cannot tell *did the thing* from *did not understand you*, against M3's server surfacing a shell imperative to a browser. **Q-0110 is one wider than GA-4 named**, because a third zero was routed to it later by a source comment, so the obligation grew in the code after the instruction that created it. Its iteration 2 is also the third instance of *"a retry on an unchanged tree cannot rule its own blocker"* and the first where the second pass found something anyway, by re-examining whether two of iteration 1's three blockers were blockers at all. **Q-0102 was parked at `p2`**, the first ticket here retired by measuring its own premise dead rather than by shipping anything: 0 failures in 36 local sweeps and 0 in 28 CI sweep cells across fourteen consecutive green runs, against an opening claim that *"every push is red or lucky"* which was an inference made while `main` stood 89 commits ahead of `origin/main` — Q-0105's subject, and now the second thing that gap hid. Its GO-1 is what parked it: at that rate no fix can be shown red before green, and no step in the requirements flow can produce a failure rate, so a run would have been the sixteenth appearance of a loop handed work no agent in it can perform — **recognised before the money was spent, for the first time**. The sighting stands, the reopening threshold is written into the ticket, and the `p1` half that survives is that a flaky oracle trains the reader to re-run until green. **Q-0103 ran the cutover**, so M2's done-when reads one required suite rather than two and Q-0009's *"what is not done"* is discharged: `spike/` is deleted with its CI job, the three port-freeze jobs, `harness/port-charter.md` and `packages/core/src/spike-parity.test.ts`, and Q-0010's second inherited obligation — the binary half of that suite — is recorded as landed in Q-0095 and Q-0101.   1391	    than closed by it. `workTreeProbe` asks `repositoryAt` after a git fatal, and `repositoryAt`
  1392	    collapses every failure of `rev-parse --resolve-git-dir` to `false` — so a **malformed gitfile**
  1393	    at `repoDir/.git` and an **unreadable `.git`** both read as absence and render nothing, giving
  1394	    silence a second meaning it was not given. That is against *"The board reports push lag, and
  1395	    never a CI conclusion"* (2026-09-06), whose rule is that silence means only that git answered.
  1396	    **AC-3 is met and this is not an unmet criterion**, its binding clause being *none of them
  1397	    reaches `pushed`*, which is why E-1 ruled a residual; the ticket exists because the residual sits
  1398	    on a just-ruled entry's central property.
  1399	    **E-1's third case is closed as unclosable and must not be reopened without new evidence.** A
  1400	    project root *below* a repository git refuses cannot be caught without either git's translated
  1401	    prose, which may never decide a state, or a reimplementation of git's upward discovery walk,
  1402	    whose verdict would then depend on where a fixture happens to sit — refused by *"A test's verdict
  1403	    is a property of the commit, not of the checkout or the account"* (2026-08-30).
  1404	    **The remaining two are a design question, which is why they are a ticket and not a fix.** The
  1405	    obvious instrument is a filesystem existence check at `repoDir/.git`, and it collides with
  1406	    *"Membership is a git question, not a filesystem one"* (2026-08-28). Whether that collision is
  1407	    real, or whether that entry is scoped to `turbo-inputs.test.ts` and its argument about what turbo
  1408	    hashes has no analogue here, is the work — Q-0090's E-1 is the precedent for ruling exactly that

  1410	  - Q-0110 The CLI reports success three times over for a non-success. *(`reviewed` 2026-09-08,
  1411	    implemented by hand with its decision entry, and **the title is now wrong in a way worth
  1412	    keeping**: two of the three were ruled correct.)* See *"What an exit code may claim, and the
  1413	    three zeros it was asked about"* (2026-09-08). **One changed**: an unknown or absent command
  1414	    prints the help and reports `ERROR`, where `quorum help`, `--help` and `-h` print the same text
  1415	    and report success — help on purpose is success, help by accident is not. **Two were ratified.**
  1416	    `regressed` keeps `SUCCESS` because an exit code reports a run's **disposition** and not its
  1417	    verdict, and `lifecycle.ts` sides `regressed` with `completed` in every question it asks of a
  1418	    terminal status — `advancesStage` and `returnsWorktrees` are both `completed || regressed`, and
  1419	    `restoresBranch` excludes it, so a regressed run is not rolled back. Bare `quorum adapters`
  1420	    keeps `SUCCESS` because its own last line disclaims being the gate — *"presence only … run
  1421	    `quorum adapters --probe` before a real run"* — so it is a **report** like `board` and `runs`,
  1422	    while `--probe` is the **check**, and that one changed: an unusable login is now `ERROR`, set
  1423	    rather than thrown so the listing still prints. One command, two contracts, the shape
  1424	    `git diff` and `git diff --exit-code` already have.
  1425	    **Reviewed cross-vendor, two rounds.** Round 1 returned two majors, both real. `askedForHelp`
  1426	    scanned the raw argv, so `quorum nonsense --help` exited 0 **against the ruling it implemented**
  1427	    — help must be the whole request, and four mixed cases now pin it. And the entry cited a
  1428	    `finished` predicate that **no longer exists**: decision 076 split it into three, which the
  1429	    correction turned into a stronger argument than the one it replaced. Round 2 returned a major
  1430	    and a nit, both the same class — four authority sites still calling the ratified zeros
  1431	    *"preserved defects"* awaiting this ticket, in `exit.ts`, `adapters.ts`, `main.test.ts` and this

  1434	    successor for the exit table's two zeros"*; searched 2026-09-07, `GA-4` appears only inside

  1436	    where nothing succeeded, each carrying a `Why: preserved defect` line that routes here by name:
  1437	    an **unknown or absent command** prints help and returns (`main.ts:85–92`, Q-0090 AC-6),
  1438	    **`regressed`** shares `completed`'s `SUCCESS` (`exit.ts:61–63`, Q-0090 AC-4(c)), and
  1439	    **`quorum adapters` with both CLIs absent** (`adapters.ts:20–23`, Q-0099 AC-8(c)). **GA-4 named
  1440	    two of the three** — the adapters zero was routed here later by a source comment Q-0099 wrote —
  1441	    so the obligation grew in the code after the instruction that created it, and reading GA-4 alone
  1442	    under-scopes the ticket by one. The mechanism behind the `regressed` zero is already closed:
  1443	    `EXIT_CODE_FOR_STATUS` is keyed by the terminal-status union so a seventh status fails to
  1444	    compile, which is how that one got in. **What remains is the value, which is what a compiler
  1445	    cannot check.** The vocabulary is closed at five — 0, 1, 2, 3, 130, with `ExitCode` a union — so
  1446	    the decision is *choose among five, three times*, and the three answers need not agree: an unmet
  1447	    precondition, a misunderstood request and a run whose outcome moved the ticket backwards are
  1448	    three different claims. GA-4's *"none is opened for colour policy until a README exists"* stays.
